Q1. A does \(\frac{4}{9}\) of a job in 9 days. What is the time required for the complete job?
A. 18 days
B. 81 days
C. 4 days
D. 20 1/4 days
Answer: 20 1/4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=9\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=20.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{4}{9}=20.25
\]
Answer: 20 1/4 days

---

Q2. A tank is filled by a pipe taking 12 hours and emptied by another pipe taking 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled if both are opened?
A. 22 hours
B. 18 hours
C. 27 hours
D. 17 hours
Answer: 18 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_basic_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 hours

---

Q3. A takes 24 days to finish a work and B takes 6 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 30 days
B. 6 days
C. 15 days
D. 4 4/5 days
Answer: 4 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,6)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/24=1,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{1+4}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 6}{24+6}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

---

Q4. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 12 days. In how many days can C alone complete it?
A. 24 days
B. 28 4/5 days
C. 29 days
D. 27 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q5. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 30 days. After 8 days, the number of people becomes 40. For how many more days will the food last?
A. 19 1/2 days
B. 20 1/2 days
C. 16 1/2 days
D. 13 1/5 days
Answer: 16 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=16.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 16 1/2 days

---

Q6. Two workers A and B have efficiencies 3:4. If both together finish the job in 12 days, in how many days can A alone finish it?
A. 28 days
B. 9 days
C. 19 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 28 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(3+4)\times 12
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]
Answer: 28 days

---

Q7. A team has 3 men and 4 women. They finish 4, 3 boxes per person per day respectively. How many days are needed for 360 boxes?
A. 19 days
B. 14 days
C. 15 days
D. 22 1/2 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{360}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 15 days

---

Q8. A pipe takes 12 hours to fill a tank from empty. The tank is already one-fourth full. In how many hours will it be completely filled?
A. 5 hours
B. 8 hours
C. 10 4/5 hours
D. 9 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

---

\[
x=\frac{120-60}{6}
\]

x=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:

Q10. A completes checking 40 answer sheets over 8 days. What is the one-day count of answer sheets?

r=\frac{40}{8}
\]

Shortcut / Exam Method:

Q11. A filling pipe fills a tank in 12 hours and a leak empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled when both work together?
A. 16 hours
B. 19 hours
C. 26 hours
D. 20 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

---

Q12. A pump sends 12 litres of water per minute into a tank. How many litres will it send in 40 minutes?
A. 476 litres
B. 480 litres
C. 481 litres
D. 720 litres
Answer: 480 litres
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_capacity_leakage_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=12\times 40
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=480
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 480 litres

Q13. Water is added at 12 litres in the first hour and removed at 5 litres in the next hour, and this continues. How many hours will a 60-litre tank take to fill?
A. 20 1/3 hours
B. 24 1/2 hours
C. 13 1/3 hours
D. 16 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

---

Q14. A alone can complete a work in 10 days and B alone in 8 days. What is the ratio of work done by them in the same time?
A. 8:18
B. 10:8
C. 18:8
D. 4:5
Answer: 4:5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{10}:\frac{1}{8}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W_A:W_B=8:10
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
W_A:W_B=4:5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=4:5
\]
Answer: 4:5

---

Q15. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 36 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 26 days
B. 20 days
C. 17 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q16. Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and pipe B can fill it in 18 hours. Both pipes are used, and Pipe A is closed after 2 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 18 hours
B. 15 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 16 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

---

Q17. Food is sufficient for 20 people for 45 days. After 5 days, the number of people becomes 30. For how many more days will the food last?

B. 32 2/3 days

D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]

\[
D=\frac{900-20\times 5}{30}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 days

Q18. 10 workers complete the same job in 15 days at 8 hours a day. How many workers are required to complete the same job in 20 days at 6 hours a day?
A. 14 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 15 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q19. A and B start a work together. A can complete it in 30 days and B in 12 days. After 3 days, A leaves. In how many days will the work be completed in all?
A. 10 4/5 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 8 4/5 days
D. 16 4/5 days
Answer: 10 4/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(6+15)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{117}{15}=10.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 10 4/5 days

---

Q20. A works on the first day, B on the second and C on the third, then the order repeats. They complete 6, 6 and 4 forms on their days. In how many days will 72 forms be completed?
A. 17 1/3 days
B. 10 2/3 days
C. 13 1/3 days
D. 14 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+6+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

---

Q21. A needs 15 days for a work and A with B needs 12 days. In how many days can B do the work alone?
A. 60 days
B. 27 days
C. 13 1/2 days
D. 3 days
Answer: 60 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
B=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 15}{15-12}=60
\]
Answer: 60 days

---

Q23. A team completes 3 files per day for 2 days and then 2 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 12 files?
A. 4 days
B. 2 1/2 days
C. 5 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{6}{2}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q24. If a work is completed in 15 days and 4 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 11/15
B. 4/15
C. 15/11
D. 11/4
Answer: 11/15
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{4}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{11}{15}=11/15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-4}{15}=11/15
\]
Answer: 11/15

Q27. After a delay, 60 of 300 records are completed by 10 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 10 days?
A. 20 workers
B. 11.2 workers
C. 14 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 14 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 14 workers

---

Q28. A job earns ₹7200 for A and B. If their work shares are in the ratio 8:12, what is A's wage?
A. ₹2160
B. ₹2880
C. ₹2883
D. ₹2875
Answer: ₹2880
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_wage_partial_time
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{8}{20}\times 7200
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=2880
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹2880

---

Q29. A can do a work in 24 days and B in 6 days. After A works for 4 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 6 2/5 days
B. 8 days
C. 5 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=5\times 4,\quad R=120-20
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{100}{5+20}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{120-5\times 4}{5+20}
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

Q32. 10 workers working 8 hours per day finish the job in 15 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 20 days?
A. 14 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

\]

Q34. The three workers A, B and C complete a work in 6 days. If A and B together complete it in 12 days, how many days will C alone need?

\]

\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]

Q46. In a joint job, A alone needs 15 days and B alone needs 30 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?

\]
Answer: 12 1/3 days

Q75. 18 farm workers can harvest a field in 8 days by working 5 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 10 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 27 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q88. A and B can separately complete a work in 12 and 24 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 13 1/3 days
B. 10 1/3 days
C. 15 1/3 days
D. 12 2/5 days
Answer: 10 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
10(T-2)+5(T-3)=120
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=10\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 10 1/3 days

Q92. Without leakage, a tank fills in 12 hours. With leakage, it fills in 18 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?
A. 35 hours
B. 32 hours
C. 36 hours
D. 43 1/5 hours
Answer: 36 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[

\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 36 hours

Answer: 4 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,12)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/12=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+2}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 12}{8+12}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Q113. A, B and C together complete a work in 10 days. A and B together can complete it in 12 days. In how many days will C alone complete the work?

\]

Q119. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 24 hours
B. 21 hours
C. 28 4/5 hours
D. 20 hours
Answer: 24 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 24 hours

Q124. 16 workers working 6 hours per day finish the job in 12 days. If the daily working time is 8 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 18 days?
A. 24 workers
B. 11 workers
C. 8 workers
D. 16 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
16\times 12\times 6=M_2\times 18\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{16\times 12\times 6}{18\times 8}=8
\]
Answer: 8 workers

Q125. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 24 days and B alone needs 15 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?
A. 16 1/2 days
B. 15 3/4 days
C. 9 3/4 days
D. 13 3/4 days
Answer: 13 3/4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+8)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{94}{8}=13.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 13 3/4 days

Q126. The order is A, then B, then C, and it repeats. They build 10, 4 and 4 metres of wall on their turns. How many days are needed for 90 metres?

C. 19 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+4+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 15 days

\]

\[

\]

\]

Q149. A pipe transfers 15 litres of water every minute. How many litres are transferred in 30 minutes?
A. 360 litres
B. 456 litres
C. 449 litres
D. 450 litres
Answer: 450 litres
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_two_tanks_transfer
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=15\times 30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=450
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 450 litres

Q150. 12 farm workers can harvest a field in 10 days by working 8 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 16 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 12 workers
B. 19 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q151. Machines A, B and C produce 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour respectively. If they work for 8 hours, how many parts will be produced in total?

\[
O=12\times 8+15\times 8+18\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 360 items

Q152. The three workers A, B and C complete a work in 8 days. If A and B together complete it in 16 days, how many days will C alone need?
A. 35 days
B. 32 days
C. 25 3/5 days
D. 36 days
Answer: 32 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_unknown_worker_from_team
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{16}-\frac{1}{32}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 32 days

Q153. 2 men and 3 women work together. A man packs 4 boxes per day and a woman packs 2 boxes per day. How many days are needed for 300 boxes?

Q154. For 5 days, a worker finishes 15 items daily. Later the worker finishes 12 items daily. In how many days will 180 items be completed?
A. 14 3/4 days
B. 18 3/4 days
C. 16 1/2 days
D. 13 3/4 days
Answer: 13 3/4 days

Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:

\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]

\[
T=5+\frac{180-15\times 5}{12}
\]

T=13.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 13 3/4 days

Q162. A food stock lasts 30 days for 20 people. How many days will it last for 25 people?
A. 26 days
B. 28 4/5 days
C. 24 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 30=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 30}{25}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q163. In a joint job, A alone needs 10 days and B alone needs 8 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?
A. 7 days
B. 10 days
C. 8 3/4 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 7 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
28(T-2)+35(T-3)=280
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=7
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 7 days

Q165. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 4:5. Together they complete a work in 10 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

D. 22 1/2 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q167. A tank is filled by a pipe in 10 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 20 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 16 hours
B. 24 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 19 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

\]

\]

Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=6\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=13.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=6\div \frac{4}{9}=13.5
\]
Answer: 13 1/2 days

Q187. A and B can separately complete a job in 12 days and 18 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 12 days
B. 7 1/5 days
C. 15 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 7 1/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,18)=36
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=36/12=3,\quad B=36/18=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{36}{3+2}=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 18}{12+18}=7.2
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

Q188. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 20 days. In how many days can C alone complete it?

Q194. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. A drain pipe can empty the tank in 36 hours. If both are open, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 28 hours
B. 36 hours
C. 35 hours
D. 30 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

Q199. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 20 days and B alone takes 18 days. How long does the job take?
A. 13 days

C. 12 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 12 days

Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
T=12
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 12 days

Q201. Workers A, B and C work one after another daily. A repairs 8 metres of road, B 4 metres and C 5 metres. How many days are needed for 72 metres?

\]

W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{8}:\frac{1}{18}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W_A:W_B=18:8
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
W_A:W_B=9:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=9:4
\]
Answer: 9:4

Q225. A job is finished by 12 workers in 10 days when each works 8 hours daily. How many workers are needed for 16 days at 6 hours daily?

\[

\]

Q226. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 30 days and B alone takes 24 days. After 5 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?

D. 24 days
Answer: 20 days

Family: tw_forward_leave

\[

\]

\[
W_1=(4+5)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{75}{5}=20
\]

\[

\]

Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=12\times \frac{3}{1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=12\div \frac{1}{3}=36
\]
Answer: 36 days

Q261. A takes 10 days to finish a work and B takes 30 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 20 days
B. 40 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Q262. A, B and C together take 10 days. A and B together take 20 days. In how many days can C alone complete it?

Q263. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 24 days. After 8 days, the number of people becomes 36. For how many more days will the food last?

D. 16 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change

\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]

\[
D=\frac{720-30\times 8}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q267. 18 workers can build a road stretch in 8 days by working 5 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 10 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 27 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q268. A and B work together at first. A's time is 8 days and B's time is 12 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished in all?
A. 8 days
B. 6 days
C. 1 day
D. 7 1/2 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(15+10)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{20}{10}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q269. The order is A, then B, then C, and it repeats. They build 6, 5 and 5 metres of wall on their turns. How many days are needed for 90 metres?

C. 16 4/5 days
D. 13 4/5 days
Answer: 16 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+5+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=16.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 16 4/5 days

\]

Answer: 11 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{180}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=11
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 11 workers

\]
Answer: 8 3/4 days

\]

\]
Answer: 30 hours

\]

Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 20}{20-12}=30
\]
Answer: 30 days

Q338. After a delay, 100 of 300 records are completed by 10 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 12 days?

\]

\[

\]

\[
N_{extra}=7
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 7 workers

Q339. A starts a work and B joins after 4 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 12 and 18 days. What is the total time taken?

Answer: 8 4/5 days

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q340. A and B work by turns. A starts and paints 6 metres per turn; B paints 5 metres. How many days will 90 metres take?

C. 19 3/5 days
D. 12 1/3 days
Answer: 16 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_terminal_overshoot_cycle
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]

\[
W_{cycle}=6+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 days

\]
Answer: 8 days

\]

Q409. A printer prints 120 pages per hour for 5 hours. If only 95% of the pages are accepted, how many pages are accepted?
A. 855 pages
B. 570 pages
C. 566 pages
D. 571 pages
Answer: 570 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_work_quality_rejection
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
O_a=O\times \frac{p}{100}
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
O_a=120\times 5\times \frac{95}{100}
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
O_a=570
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O_a=gross\times p/100
\]
Answer: 570 pages

---

Q410. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 2:3, and the efficiencies of B and C are in the ratio 3:4. What is the ratio A:B:C?
A. 2:3:4:2
B. 2:3:4:3
C. 2:3:4
D. 4:3:2
Answer: 2:3:4
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
B_c
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
LCM(3,3)=3
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
A:B:C=2:3:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=2:3:4
\]
Answer: 2:3:4

---

Q411. A team can complete a job in 24 days. If it works for 6 days, what fraction of the job will still be unfinished?
A. 4/3
B. 1/4
C. 3/4
D. 3/1
Answer: 3/4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{24}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{18}{24}=3/4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{24-6}{24}=3/4
\]
Answer: 3/4

---

Q412. A team of 12 workers has completed 72 items from a 240-item order. With 12 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 0.5 workers
B. 1 worker
C. 1.5 workers
D. 2 workers
Answer: 2 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{168}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 2 workers

---

Q413. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A prints 10 pages per turn and B prints 5 pages per turn. In how many days will 72 pages be printed?
A. 11 3/4 days
B. 9 2/5 days
C. 12 2/5 days
D. 15 2/5 days
Answer: 9 2/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=9.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 9 2/5 days

---

Q414. A and B can separately complete a job in 20 days and 30 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 25 days
B. 12 days
C. 20 days
D. 50 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,30)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/20=3,\quad B=60/30=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+2}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 30}{20+30}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q415. A job would be completed in 6 days of work. Due to a 1 day stoppage, in how many calendar days is it completed?
A. 13 days
B. 5 3/5 days
C. 7 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 7 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{120}{15+5}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+1=7
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 7 days

\]

Q422. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 36 days. For how many days will the same food last for 30 people?
A. 31 days
B. 39 days
C. 27 days
D. 36 days

Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 36=30\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 36}{30}
\]
Answer: 36 days

Q423. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 20 days. How long does the job take?
A. 9 days
B. 7 1/5 days
C. 12 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
18(T-2)+9(T-3)=180
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q444. A alone takes 24 days and B alone takes 6 days. A works alone for 4 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?

B. 8 days

D. 6 2/5 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium

\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{20}{1+4}=8
\]

\[
T=4+\frac{24-1\times 4}{1+4}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q450. A can finish a work in 6 days and B in 15 days. They work together for 3 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?

B. 12 1/2 days
C. 9 days
D. 7 1/2 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(10+4)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{18}{4}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

T=9
\]

\]

Q462. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 18 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?

\]
Answer: 9 3/5 days

Q469. One worker can do a job in 30 days and another can do it in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 27 days
B. 54 days
C. 24 days
D. 13 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,24)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/30=4,\quad B=120/24=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{4+5}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 24}{30+24}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q483. A group of 24 people has food for 30 days. They consume it for 6 days, and then the group becomes 30. How many days will the remaining food last?
A. 14 2/5 days
B. 24 1/5 days
C. 19 1/5 days
D. 21 1/5 days
Answer: 19 1/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_remaining_stock
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-24\times 6}{30}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=19.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 19 1/5 days

Q484. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 20 days. After 3 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?
A. 21 days
B. 14 days
C. 10 days
D. 17 days
Answer: 14 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(18+9)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{99}{9}=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 14 days

Q485. The order is A, then B, then C, and it repeats. They build 8, 4 and 4 metres of wall on their turns. How many days are needed for 60 metres?
A. 8 days
B. 15 days
C. 16 1/2 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 11 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+4+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=11
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 11 days

Q486. If a work is completed in 15 days and 10 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 2/3
B. 1/2
C. 1/3
D. 3/1
Answer: 1/3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{10}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{5}{15}=1/3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-10}{15}=1/3
\]
Answer: 1/3

Q488. 10 workers have packed 60 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 6 workers
B. 8 workers
C. 4 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{180}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

Q489. A starts a work and B joins after 2 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 30 and 18 days. What is the total time taken?

B. 14 1/2 days
C. 16 1/2 days
D. 12 1/2 days
Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:

\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{84}{3+5}=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{90-3\times 2}{3+5}
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q494. 30 people have food for 36 days. If the number of people becomes 40, for how many days will the food last?

Q495. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 6 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?
A. 11 2/3 days
B. 3 2/3 days
C. 5 days
D. 6 2/3 days
Answer: 6 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
5(T-2)+10(T-3)=60
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[

\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
